---

You've already got the key clue in the event:

> `failed to fetch anonymous token … 401 Unauthorized` from `https://fitfileregistry.azurecr.io/oauth2/token…`

That means the node/container runtime is trying to pull **without valid ACR credentials** (anonymous), and ACR is rejecting it. So this is **not** a bad tag or image format problem—this is **auth to ACR**.

Here's the fastest way to isolate + fix on AKS.

## 1) Confirm whether AKS is Attached to that ACR (most common)

If AKS isn't attached (or attachment broke), pulls will 401.

Run:

Then ensure the kubelet identity has **AcrPull** on the ACR:

If you **don't** see `AcrPull`, fix it:

If you prefer the simple "attach" command (often easiest / idempotent):

Then restart the failing pod (or just delete it and let ReplicaSet recreate):

## 2) If You're Using a Private ACR without AKS Attachment: Use an imagePullSecret

Your pod spec shows **no `imagePullSecrets`**, and it's using the **default** service account.

Check:

If empty, create a docker-registry secret and reference it:

Then patch either:

- the **Deployment** (recommended):

or the **default service account** for that namespace:

## 3) Sanity Checks to Rule out "image Doesn't exist"

Auth is failing first, but still worth confirming the tag exists:

If that returns nothing, you've got a _second_ issue (wrong tag), but the 401 must be fixed regardless.

## 4) Why only This App is Failing?

Because other pods are running **doesn't prove** ACR auth is correct—those images may be:

- already cached on nodes, or
- coming from a different registry, or
- public.

Quick check what registries are in use:

## 5) One More AKS-specific Check: Node Pool Identity Differences

Your pod is scheduled on `aks-system-…`. If you have multiple node pools, confirm the kubelet identity (or permissions) applies cluster-wide. Usually it does, but if this is an unusual setup (custom runtime, old SP-based cluster, etc.) it can differ.

---

### If You Paste just These 3 Outputs, I Can Tell You Exactly Which Fix Path is Right

But you don't need to wait for that—**on AKS the most likely fix is `az aks update --attach-acr fitfileregistry`** (or adding `AcrPull` to the kubelet identity) and then recreate the pod.

Yep—you _already_ have the pull secret in **lca-prd-2**:

- `lca-prd-2/fitfile-image-pull-secret` (`kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson`)

…but your failing pod is using:

- **ServiceAccount:**`default`
- and there's **no `imagePullSecrets`** on the pod/deployment (from your `describe` output)

So it's simply **not using the secret**, hence the kubelet attempts **anonymous** pull → **401**.

### Fast Fix (pick one)

## Option A (best): Patch the Deployment to Use the Secret

## Option B: Attach it to the Default ServiceAccount in that Namespace

This makes _any_ pod using `default` automatically use the pull secret.

---

## Quick Confirm before/after

You want to see `fitfile-image-pull-secret` in at least one of those.

---

## Why it Differed from "other namespaces"

Those namespaces either:

- set `spec.template.spec.imagePullSecrets` in the workload manifests, **or**
- patched their service account defaults, **or**
- use a different service account that includes the secret.

---

### ArgoCD Angle (so it Doesn't revert)

If you do this patch manually, ArgoCD may flip it back unless the Git/Helm chart includes it.

In Helm values (typical pattern), you want something like one of these in the chart values for that app:

or (if chart supports it):

If you tell me whether this frontend is deployed via a Helm chart (and which chart), I'll point you to the exact values key it expects.

For now, do **Option A** and the pod should start pulling immediately.

---

Two separate problems are showing up here:

1. **You're trying to reach a `ClusterIP` service from outside the cluster (or at least not from inside the cluster network).**
2. **`lca.fitfile.net` literally doesn't resolve (NXDOMAIN)** so even if ingress is fine, your client can't find it.

Let's walk it down quickly.

## A. Why Curl 10.2.0.235 Hangs

`10.2.0.235` is a **ClusterIP**. That IP is only reachable **inside the cluster network** (and sometimes only from pods, depending on how your AKS/VNet routing is set up). If you're on your laptop, or not on the AKS VNet with proper routing, it'll just hang.

First check whether the service has any endpoints (this also catches selector/port issues):

### If ENDPOINTS is Empty

Then the service isn't selecting any ready pods (label mismatch) or the pods aren't Ready.

Common causes:

- Service selector doesn't match pod labels
- Pod is Running but not Ready (readiness probe failing)
- Wrong `targetPort`

You can confirm selectors vs labels:

### Quick Functional Test (works even without ingress/DNS)

Port-forward and hit it locally:

If that works, the app/service are fine and the issue is purely ingress/DNS/routing.

---

## B. Why lca.fitfile.net Fails: NXDOMAIN

NXDOMAIN means **no DNS record exists** (or you're querying a resolver that can't see a private zone).

So even before we talk ingress, confirm whether this is supposed to be:

- **Public DNS** (Route53/Cloudflare/etc), or
- **Private DNS** (Azure Private DNS Zone / internal only)

From your output, your machine is using systemd-resolved (127.0.0.53) and it can't find it, which strongly suggests the record isn't present publicly.

### Check if You even Have an Ingress and what Address it Got

You're looking for an ingress controller service of type **LoadBalancer** with an **EXTERNAL-IP**. If it's `<pending>` or missing, DNS won't work.

Typical nginx ingress service is something like:

- `ingress-nginx-controller` (type LoadBalancer) → gives an external IP

Once you have that IP, your DNS A record for `lca.fitfile.net` must point to it.

---

## C. The Most Likely Immediate Explanation

- Your frontend service is **ClusterIP**, so direct curl to `10.2.0.235` from your machine is not a valid access method unless you're truly inside the cluster network.
- Your intended hostname **doesn't exist in DNS** right now (NXDOMAIN), so browser/curl can't reach it.

---

## Send Me These 3 Command Outputs and I'll Tell You Exactly What's Broken

In the meantime, use the **port-forward** test above to confirm the frontend is actually serving traffic. If port-forward works, we focus 100% on ingress + DNS.

---

Nice—this proves the **frontend pod + service are healthy inside the cluster**:

- Service `ff-lca-prd-2-frontend-frontend` selects the pod ✅
- Endpoint exists: `10.0.1.19:4000` ✅
- Pod is `1/1 Running` ✅

So the "can't reach frontend" is **not** an app/service problem. It's access:

### 1) Your Curl 10.2.0.235 Hang is Expected

`10.2.0.235` is a **ClusterIP**. You generally **cannot curl it from your workstation** (unless you're on the AKS VNet with explicit routing). That's why it just sits there.

**Quick proof/test from your machine:**

If this works, your frontend is serving traffic fine.

### 2) Your DNS is also Broken: lca.fitfile.net = NXDOMAIN

That's a separate (and decisive) issue: the hostname doesn't exist in DNS from where you're querying.

So to make the app reachable "normally", you need an **Ingress / LoadBalancer** + a **DNS record** pointing to it.

## Next: Check Ingress Controller and Ingress Objects

Run these and paste output:

### What We're Looking for

- An ingress controller service (often `ingress-nginx-controller`) of type **LoadBalancer** with an **EXTERNAL-IP**
- An `Ingress` in `lca-prd-2` that routes `lca.fitfile.net` (and likely path `/fitfile`) to your service

If you **don't** have a LoadBalancer EXTERNAL-IP, there's nothing for DNS to point at.
If you **do** have one, then you need to create/fix the DNS record:

- Public DNS: create an **A record** `lca.fitfile.net -> <external ip>`
- Private DNS: ensure your client is using the right resolver / VPN / private zone linkage

## Quick "in cluster" Curl (to Fully Isolate networking)

This tests service reachability from inside the cluster without port-forward:

### One More Thing: why This Changed "now"

Fixing image pulls caused a **new pod** to come up with a **new IP** (10.0.1.19). That's fine because Services/Ingress abstract it. Your real blocker is:

- you were never going to hit ClusterIP directly from your laptop, and
- your DNS record isn't present (NXDOMAIN).

Send the ingress-nginx + ingress outputs and I'll tell you exactly what's missing and what to set DNS to.

---

Perfect—your curl test proves the **ingress + frontend are working**. You got `HTTP/2 200` back from `GET /fitfile`, routed via ingress IP `10.0.0.203`.

So the reason you "can't reach the app" is **100% DNS** (and one smaller TLS detail).

## 1) The Real Blocker: DNS is NXDOMAIN

Your earlier `nslookup lca.fitfile.net` returned **NXDOMAIN**. That means there is **no DNS record** (public or in the resolver you're using).

You already have the ingress controller exposed at:

- `ingress-nginx-controller` EXTERNAL-IP: **10.0.0.203**
- Ingress hosts: **lca.fitfile.net**

✅ Fix: create DNS record for `lca.fitfile.net` → `10.0.0.203`

### Important: 10.0.0.203 is a Private IP

That's in RFC1918 space, so this must be **private DNS** (Azure Private DNS zone, internal corporate DNS, etc.). If you create a public DNS record pointing to 10.0.0.203, it won't work for the public internet.

So you need one of these:

- **Private DNS zone** (Azure Private DNS) for `fitfile.net` (or delegated subzone) linked to the VNet your clients use, **or**
- **Corporate DNS** record, **or**
- Use split-horizon DNS (internal returns 10.0.0.203, external returns public LB IP)

## 2) You're also Serving the "Fake Certificate"

Your curl shows:

- `CN=Kubernetes Ingress Controller Fake Certificate`

That means your Ingress for `lca.fitfile.net` **either doesn't specify a TLS secret**, or the secret is missing/misnamed, so nginx falls back to its default self-signed cert.

That won't stop basic access (your curl used `-k`), but browsers will complain hard.

---

## What to Do now (concrete steps)

## A) Make DNS Resolve (fastest verification)

On your machine, add a temporary hosts entry to confirm end-to-end without waiting on DNS:

If that works, you've proven DNS is the only missing piece.

## B) Create the Correct DNS Record (real fix)

Wherever your internal DNS is managed, add:

- **A record:**`lca.fitfile.net` → `10.0.0.203`

If you're using **Azure Private DNS**, it'll look like:

- Private DNS Zone: `fitfile.net`
- Record set: `lca` (A) = `10.0.0.203`
- Link zone to the VNet where your client machines resolve from (VPN/VNet linkage)

(If you tell me whether you're using Azure Private DNS or something else, I'll give the exact az cli commands.)

## C) Fix TLS on the Ingress (recommended next)

Find which ingress is actually serving `/fitfile` (you have _multiple_ ingresses for the same host, which is risky):

You currently have **two frontend ingresses**:

- `ff-lca-prd-2-frontend-frontend-e1d11-default-ingress`
- `ff-lca-prd-2-frontend-frontend-ingress`

…and also ingresses for ffcloud + fitconnect on the same host.

That can work, but you must ensure **paths don't overlap ambiguously**.

### Inspect the Frontend Ingress Rules + TLS

Look for:

- `tls:` section
- `secretName: <something>`
- paths like `/fitfile` and whether there's also `/` anywhere

If you want a proper cert:

- use cert-manager (Let's Encrypt) **only if** this is publicly reachable, or
- use an internal CA / imported cert secret for private ingress

At minimum, set `spec.tls[0].secretName` and make sure the secret exists in `lca-prd-2`.

---

## One More Potential Landmine: Multiple Ingresses Same Host

You have 4 ingresses in `lca-prd-2` for the same host (`lca.fitfile.net`). That's okay _if_ they each handle distinct paths, but it's easy to accidentally create a `/` catch-all that steals traffic.

Quick check:

If any ingress has path `/` with `pathType: Prefix`, it can override expectations.

---

## Bottom line

- **Ingress is fine** (your `--resolve` curl proves it).
- **DNS is missing** for `lca.fitfile.net` (NXDOMAIN).
- **TLS is misconfigured** (fake cert), fix next.

If you paste the `describe ingress` output for the _frontend_ ingress, I'll tell you exactly what TLS secret name it expects and whether you've got a path-collision risk.

---

---

You're seeing **two separate things**:

1. Your `curl` to `https://lca.fitfile.net/fitfile` succeeds at the TLS + HTTP layer and returns **`HTTP/2 200`**.
2. Your **ingress-nginx controller logs** are complaining that:
- some Services have **no active Endpoints** (i.e., no ready Pods backing them), and
- the referenced TLS Secret **doesn't exist**, so nginx serves the **default self-signed "Kubernetes Ingress Controller Fake Certificate"**.

Those log lines are _absolutely consistent_ with your curl output (self-signed fake cert + `-k`).

---

## Why You Can Get HTTP 200 while Nginx Says "no Active Endpoint"

A 200 does **not** prove your backend app worked. Common reasons you still get 200:

- You hit the **nginx default backend** (or some other rule) that returns 200 for that path.
- Your Ingress rule matches, but the upstream is missing, and nginx is configured/behaving in a way that still yields 200 (less common), or you're not actually hitting the Ingress you think.
- You didn't show the response body/headers—so it might be a 200 with an nginx "default backend" page or some minimal body.

First, confirm what you're actually getting:

Look for headers like `server: nginx` and any app-specific headers/body.

---

## The Two Real Problems in Your Logs

### A) "Service … Does not Have Any Active Endpoint"

This means: **the Service exists, but it has no Ready Pods matching its selector** (or the Pods are not Ready, or wrong port names).

Check endpoints and what the Service is selecting:

Then verify Pods + labels + readiness:

**Most common fixes**

- Service selector doesn't match pod labels (Helm release name changes, etc.).
- Pods exist but are **not Ready** (readinessProbe failing).
- Service points at a port name/number that doesn't exist on the Pods (`targetPort` mismatch).

---

### B) "local SSL Certificate … Was not Found. Using Default certificate"

Your Ingress references Secrets that don't exist:

- `argocd/cloudflare-tls`
- `lca-prd-2/fitfile-lca-tls`

Confirm:

**Fix options**

- If you _intend_ TLS termination at nginx: create the Secret(s):
- If you're using **cert-manager**: ensure the `Certificate` resource exists and is issuing into that secret name.
- If you're not ready for TLS yet: temporarily remove the `spec.tls:` block from the Ingress (or point it at a Secret that exists) so it stops spamming logs.

---

## Prove Which Ingress Rule is Serving Your Request

Find all ingresses for that host and see which one matches:

Also check nginx controller access logs to see the upstream / ingress name (depends on your controller config):

---

## Quick Interpretation of Your Snippets

- Your curl shows the **default fake cert**, which matches:
	`Error getting SSL certificate "…": … not found. Using default certificate`
- Your controller repeatedly says Services in `argocd` and `lca-prd-2` have **no active Endpoint**—that's almost always a Service↔Pod selector/readiness issue.
- So: **ingress is reachable**, but at least some of your backends are not actually routable yet, and TLS secrets aren't present.

---

Nice—this output clears up the mystery.

### What Your Curl is Actually Proving

You are **definitely hitting a real app**, not the nginx default backend:

- Response headers include **`x-powered-by: Next.js`**
- Body is a **Next.js HTML shell** with assets under `/fitfile/_next/…`
- CSP + assetPrefix show it's intentionally hosted at **`/fitfile`**

So your Ingress is routing `/fitfile` to a **frontend (Next.js) service** successfully.

### Why You Still See the "fake certificate"

That part is still true and independent:

Meaning: the Ingress controller is serving a cert, but **not the cert you intended** (the TLS secret referenced by the Ingress either doesn't exist, is in the wrong namespace, or the ingress points at the wrong secret name). So browsers will complain unless you use `-k`.

---

## Your Service/Endpoints Are Healthy (at Least This one)

Your service `ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service` _does_ have endpoints:

- `TargetPort: 4000/TCP`
- `Endpoints: 10.0.0.91:4000`
- EndpointSlice says `ready: true`

So this specific service is **not** in the "no active Endpoint" state _right now_.

If nginx logs still claim _this_ service has no endpoints, it usually means one of these:

- nginx is referencing a **different Service name** (very common)
- there are **multiple ingresses** for the host/path and one points to a dead service
- endpoints were missing earlier and logs are from that period (nginx is chatty)

---

## The Key Question Now: Are You Expecting /fitfile to Be the API?

Your HTML page contains config pointing to:

- `https://lca.fitfile.net/ffcloud/graphql`
- `https://lca.fitfile.net/ffcloud`

So likely:

- `/fitfile` = **frontend UI**
- `/ffcloud` = **backend API**

Try hitting the backend path explicitly:

For GraphQL you may need POST:

If that fails while `/fitfile` works, then the broken part is the **Ingress rule(s) for `/ffcloud`**, not the service you pasted.

---

## Next: Prove Which Ingress is Serving /fitfile and what Else Exists for the Same Host

Run these:

You're looking for:

- host: `lca.fitfile.net`
- paths: `/fitfile`, `/ffcloud`, maybe `/`
- which **Service** each path routes to
- any `nginx.ingress.kubernetes.io/rewrite-target` or regex paths

If there are **two ingresses** for the same host, it's very easy for one to be "working" (`/fitfile`) while another path points at a service with no endpoints.

---

## Fix TLS Properly (so You Don't Get the Fake cert)

Once you identify the Ingress that serves `lca.fitfile.net`, check its TLS block:

You need:

- the secret referenced in `spec.tls[].secretName` to **exist in the SAME namespace as the Ingress**
- and contain a valid `kubernetes.io/tls` cert for `lca.fitfile.net`

---

## One Small Thing: Your Curl: (23) Failure Writing Output to Destination

That's just because `head` closes the pipe early (curl gets a broken pipe). It's harmless. If you want it quiet:

---

If you paste the output of:

…I can point to the exact rule that's wrong (and why nginx was complaining about endpoints / missing tls secret).

---

kubectl get ingress -A | grep -F lca.fitfile.net # Then describe the candidate ingress kubectl -n lca-prd-2 describe ingress ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service-ingress lca-prd-2 ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service-ingress <none> lca.fitfile.net 10.0.0.203 80, 443 21h lca-prd-2 ff-lca-prd-2-fitconnect-ftc-ingress <none> lca.fitfile.net 10.0.0.203 80, 443 21h lca-prd-2 ff-lca-prd-2-frontend-frontend-e1d11-default-ingress <none> lca.fitfile.net 10.0.0.203 80, 443 60m lca-prd-2 ff-lca-prd-2-frontend-frontend-ingress <none> lca.fitfile.net 10.0.0.203 80, 443 60m Name: ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service-ingress Labels: <none> Namespace: lca-prd-2 Address: 10.0.0.203 Ingress Class: <none> Default backend: <default> TLS: fitfile-lca-tls terminates lca.fitfile.net Rules: Host Path Backends ---- ---- -------- lca.fitfile.net /ffcloud ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service:80 (10.0.0.91:4000) Annotations: argocd.argoproj.io/tracking-id: ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service:networking.k8s.io/Ingress:lca-prd-2/ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service-ingress kubernetes.io/ingress.class: nginx nginx.ingress.kubernetes.io/proxy-body-size: 100m Events: <none> ➜ terraform kubectl -n ingress-nginx logs deploy/ingress-nginx-controller --tail=200 | grep -F 'lca.fitfile.net' ➜ terraform

---

Yep—_this_ explains both behaviors:

- `/fitfile` works (you're hitting the **frontend ingress**)
- you still get the **"Kubernetes Ingress Controller Fake Certificate"** because you have **FOUR ingresses for the same host** (`lca.fitfile.net`), and nginx-ingress **merges** them into one server block. If _any_ of those ingresses references a missing/invalid TLS secret (or no TLS), nginx can fall back to its default/fake cert for that host.

### What You've Proven from the Describe

`ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service-ingress` is fine for routing:

- Host: `lca.fitfile.net`
- Path: `/ffcloud` → service `ff-lca-prd-2-ffcloud-service:80` → endpoint `10.0.0.91:4000`
- TLS secret: `fitfile-lca-tls`

So the _routing_ side of this ingress looks healthy.

## 1) Find Which Ingress is Breaking TLS for the Host

Run these and scan the TLS sections:

You're looking for any of these "poison pills":

- `spec.tls` missing entirely
- `spec.tls[].secretName` set to a secret that **doesn't exist**
- a different secret name than the others (and that secret is wrong)

**Fast check** (does every referenced secret exist?):

## 2) Confirm the TLS Secret is Actually a Valid TLS Secret (and Matches the hostname)

Even if the secret exists, it might be the wrong type or missing keys.

- type should be: `kubernetes.io/tls`
- both `tls.crt` and `tls.key` must be non-empty

Optional but super useful to confirm SAN/CN (pasteable into a terminal):

If SAN doesn't include `lca.fitfile.net`, browsers will still complain (though curl `-k` wouldn't care).

---

## 3) Ask Nginx-ingress why it Served the Fake Cert

This is usually very explicit in the controller logs. Search for secret/cert errors (not only host lines):

Typical smoking guns look like:

- `secret "lca-prd-2/fitfile-lca-tls" not found`
- `no PEM data found`
- `Error obtaining X509 certificate`

---

## 4) The "real fix" with Nginx-ingress: One Host, One Ingress (or Identical TLS everywhere)

Because nginx merges all ingresses with the same `host`, the safest options are:

### Option A (best): Consolidate

Create **one ingress** for `lca.fitfile.net` that contains **all paths** (`/fitfile`, `/ffcloud`, etc.) and delete the others.

### Option B: Keep Multiple Ingresses, but Make TLS Consistent

Ensure **every** ingress for `lca.fitfile.net` includes:

(And that secret exists + is valid.)

---

What you're seeing is exactly what NGINX ingress does when the **Host header doesn't match any Ingress rule**: it serves the **default backend 404**.

### Why Curl Works but the Browser Doesn't

- Your working curl test forces **Host = `lca.fitfile.net`** and **IP = `10.0.0.203`**:
	- `--resolve lca.fitfile.net:443:10.0.0.203`
	- That matches your Ingress rules (you showed multiple Ingresses with host `lca.fitfile.net`).
- Your browser request is **Host = `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`**.
	- None of your Ingress rules are for `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`, so NGINX returns **404 Not Found** (default backend).

### Your Screenshot also Shows a DNS Mismatch

In the **`privatelink.fitfile.net`** private zone you currently have:

- `lca-prd-2` → `10.0.1.10`
- `lca-prd-2-argocd` → `10.0.1.10`

That does **not** create `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`, and it also doesn't point at your ingress IP (`10.0.0.203`) anyway.

---

## Fix Options (pick one)

### Option A (cleanest): Split-horizon DNS for lca.fitfile.net

If you want the site to be accessed internally as **`https://lca.fitfile.net/fitfile`** (same hostname everywhere):

1. Create/adjust a **private DNS zone for `fitfile.net`** (or whatever you're using internally), linked to your VNet.
2. Add **A record**:
- `lca` → `10.0.0.203`

✅ No ingress changes needed.
✅ No extra TLS SAN needed (you already terminate `lca.fitfile.net`).

---

### Option B: Make Ingress Serve lca.privatelink.fitfile.net

If you truly want to browse **`https://lca.privatelink.fitfile.net/fitfile`**:

1. Create an **A record** in `privatelink.fitfile.net`:
- `lca` → `10.0.0.203` (your NGINX ingress address)
1. Update **every relevant Ingress** (frontend + ffcloud + fitconnect etc.) to include an additional host:
2. Fix TLS: your cert must cover `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` too (SAN or wildcard for `*.privatelink.fitfile.net`), otherwise the browser will complain even after routing works.

✅ Browser URL stays `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`
❗ Requires TLS + ingress changes

- `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net -> 10.0.0.203` ✅ so your browser is definitely hitting the ingress LB.
- `lca.fitfile.net -> NXDOMAIN` ✅ so only the **privatelink** hostname exists in your internal DNS.

So the 404 in the browser is because **NGINX has no Ingress rule with host `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`** (only `lca.fitfile.net`). NGINX returns its default 404.

## Fastest Fix (no DNS work): Add lca.privatelink.fitfile.net to the Ingress Host Rules + TLS

### 1) Update Your Ingresses to Match the Privatelink Host

You currently have multiple ingresses with host `lca.fitfile.net` (ffcloud, frontend, fitconnect). Any one that should serve `/fitfile` must include:

- `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`

Example pattern:

If the frontend ingress is the one that serves `/fitfile`, that's the critical one.

### 2) TLS: Your Cert Must Cover the Privatelink Hostname

Right now your ingress terminates with secret `fitfile-lca-tls` and (based on your earlier curl output) you're seeing the **NGINX fake cert** sometimes—that typically happens when the SNI host isn't covered by any matching TLS entry/cert.

You need either:

- a cert with SAN including **`lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`**, or
- a wildcard `*.privatelink.fitfile.net`, or
- put `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` under the existing cert if it supports multiple SANs.

And ensure the ingress TLS section includes the privatelink host:

---

Yep—what you're seeing is exactly what happens when the **Host header** doesn't match any Ingress rule.

- Your Ingress rules are for **`lca.fitfile.net`** (you even saw `/ffcloud` on that host).
- In the browser you're visiting **`lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`**.
- NGINX Ingress routes by `spec.rules[].host`, so **no host match ⇒ it falls back to the default backend ⇒ `404 Not Found`** (the classic "nginx 404").

That's also why your curl worked: you forced the host to `lca.fitfile.net` with `--resolve`, so the Host header matched.

## Do You "just Update the host"?

You _can_, but there are two good options—one is usually better.

---

## Best Option (recommended): Keep lca.fitfile.net as the App Hostname, Fix Private DNS

Your Helm values and the app config embedded in the HTML are clearly built around `lca.fitfile.net` (e.g. `graphqlApiUrl` / `restApiUrl` use `https://lca.fitfile.net/…`).

So the cleanest fix is:

1. Create/link a **Private DNS zone for `fitfile.net`** (not `privatelink.fitfile.net`)
2. Add **A record**: `lca` → `10.0.0.203`
3. Link that private zone to the VNET where your clients live

Then your browser can resolve `lca.fitfile.net` internally and everything matches (Ingress host, app config URLs, cert names, etc.).

---

## Alternative Option: Add lca.privatelink.fitfile.net as an Additional Host (Ingress + TLS + cert)

If you _must_ use `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` in the browser, then you need to serve **both hostnames**.

In your `values.yaml`, you currently have `host: lca.fitfile.net` and multiple places hardcoding `lca.fitfile.net` in ingress + tls + certs.

### What to Change

Add `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` everywhere you define hosts:

- `host:` (top-level)
- `fitconnect.tls.spec[].hosts[]` and `fitconnect.ingress.hosts[].hostname`
- `ffcloud.tls.spec[].hosts[]` and `ffcloud.ingress.hosts[].hostname`
- `frontend.tls.spec[].hosts[]` and `frontend.ingress.hosts[].hostname`
- `certs.certificates[].dnsNames[]` (so the issued cert includes the privatelink name)

Concretely, for each of those sections you'd either:

- replace `lca.fitfile.net` with `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`, **or**
- (better) include **both** names.

### Important Gotcha

Even if you fix the Ingress host routing, your browser will also need a **certificate valid for `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`**. Right now your cert config only includes `lca.fitfile.net`.
So make sure the cert includes both SANs, or you'll trade the NGINX 404 for a TLS name mismatch.

---

Because your app config is already pointing at `lca.fitfile.net` endpoints, I'd **fix private DNS for `lca.fitfile.net`** (recommended option) rather than renaming everything to the privatelink hostname.

You've got a **Host mismatch**, not (primarily) a path problem.

Your Ingress objects are all keyed on:

- `host: lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net`

…but your browser is hitting:

- `https://lca.privatelink.fitfile.net/fitfile`

NGINX Ingress routes by **`spec.rules[].host`** first. If the Host header doesn't match any rule, it serves the default backend, which is the classic **"404 Not Found"** you're seeing.

### Prove it in 5 Seconds

## Fix Options (pick one)

### Option A (quickest): Make DNS Match the Existing Ingress Host

Add a DNS record so that `lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net` resolves to `10.0.0.203` (A record), **or** make `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` a CNAME to `lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net`.

Then users browse `https://lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net/fitfile` (or `lca` CNAME works too if you go with CNAME and also add Host support—see below).

### Option B (also fine): Add lca.privatelink.fitfile.net as an Additional Host in the Ingress

Update your Helm/ArgoCD values so the ingress rules include **both** hosts:

- `lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net`
- `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`

…and make sure the TLS cert secret covers both names (SANs), otherwise you'll fix routing but get a cert name error.

---

## Answer to Your Question: "do I just Need to Update the host?"

**Yes—either your browser hostname must match the Ingress host, or the Ingress must include the browser hostname.** Right now they don't match, so 404 is expected.

If you tell me which URL you want to be the "official" one (`lca-prd-2…` vs `lca…`), I'll give you the exact minimal change (DNS-only vs values/ingress + cert).

Perfect—that confirms it 100%:

- `lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net` → **matches** your Ingress host → **200**
- `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` → **no matching host rule** → **NGINX default 404**

So now you just need to decide how you want users to access it.

## Fastest Fix (DNS-only)

Make `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` point at the same thing as the working host.

In your **privatelink.fitfile.net** private DNS zone, add either:

- **A record**: `lca` → `10.0.0.203`
	(simplest, but you still need Ingress host support unless you also change Ingress)

…but **note:** even if `lca` resolves to 10.0.0.203, you'll still get 404 unless the Ingress host matches `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`.

So DNS-only works _only if_ you also browse using the hostname your Ingress already expects (`lca-prd-2…`), OR you change the Ingress host(s).

## Best Practical Fix (Ingress Host alias)

Add `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net` as an additional host on the relevant ingresses (frontend + ffcloud + fitconnect), keeping the existing `lca-prd-2…` too.

That means:

- add a second `spec.rules[].host: lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`
- add it under `spec.tls[].hosts` too
- ensure the cert in `fitfile-lca-tls` has SANs for **both** hostnames (or you'll route fine but get a browser cert error)

Nice, this output is _very_ telling. You've basically finished the networking part—what's left is TLS hygiene.

### What's Working (✅)

- **Routing is correct**
	✔ Ingress host matches
	✔ Path rules are correct
	✔ Service + pod are healthy
- **NGINX is serving traffic for that host**
	(even though your grep didn't show logs, the 200 confirms it)

### The Real Problem (❌)

Your TLS certificate is **wrong for production use**:

That means:

- The ingress controller is presenting its **default self-signed "fake" cert**
- Your secret `fitfile-lca-tls` is either:
	- missing
	- empty
	- not referenced correctly
	- or does **not** contain a cert valid for `lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net`

Browsers will _not_ accept this → hence the browser failure even though `curl -k` works.

---

## Why This Happens in NGINX Ingress

NGINX does this, in order:

1. Look for a TLS secret matching the **SNI hostname**
2. Check if the cert in that secret has a matching SAN
3. If **anything** doesn't line up → fall back to
	**"Kubernetes Ingress Controller Fake Certificate"**

That's exactly what you're seeing.

---

## What You Need to Fix (pick one)

### ✅ Option 1—Fix the TLS Secret (best / correct)

Ensure `fitfile-lca-tls` contains a cert with **both SANs**:

- `lca-prd-2.privatelink.fitfile.net`
- (optionally) `lca.privatelink.fitfile.net`

Check the secret:

Then decode:

If the hostnames aren't there → browsers will _always_ fail.
